AGENDA 
In accordance with the requirements of the Open Meetings Law, please be advised that this meeting is 
being Audio/Video Recorded. The agenda lists all the topics which may be discussed at the meeting and 
are those reasonably anticipated by the Chair. Votes may be taken as a result of these discussions. Not 
all items listed may in fact be discussed and other items not listed may also be brought up for 
discussion to the extent permitted by Open Meeting Law. Please address all comments to the chair.  
JOINT EXECUTIVE SESSION: Motion to Enter into Joint Executive Session with the Select Board 
and re-enter Open Session after exiting Executive Session pursuant to MGL c. 30 §21 (a)(3) to discuss 
strategy with respect to litigation if an open meeting may have detrimental effect on the litigating 
position of the Commission, and the Chair of the Commission so declares, namely Town of Lunenburg 
v. Latour, Worcester Superior Court Docket No. 2385CV01189 
 
Meeting schedule: Next meeting is January 8th, 2025, at 7:00 PM by Zoom platform  
 
ADJOURN

Conservation Commission 
 
Mary Wilson, Chair                                                                                                                           Tel: (978)582-4146 
Jennifer Pewtherer, Vice Chair                                                                                                          960 Massachusetts Ave 
Richard Bursch                                                                                                                                  Lunenburg, MA 01462 
Jeffrey Viviano                                                                     Sophia Dorow

-Vacancy-                                                                                                                                              Conservation Assistant 
 
 
January 8, 2025, 7:00PM, ZOOM REMOTE 
Minutes 
 
Roll Call: Chris S., Mike L., Jeff V ., Jenny P., Rich B., Mary W. and Sophi a Dorow (Admin.)  
Call to Order at 7 :01pm by Mary W. 
Announcements & Public Comment -none  
 
Minutes of previous meetings – 11/6/24 
Motion: Rich B., Second: Jenny P. 
To approve the minutes of 11/6/24.  
Roll Call V ote: Chris-aye, Mike-aye, Jeff-aye, Rich-aye, Jenny -aye and Mary -aye. 
 
APPOINTMENTS 
 
NEW PUBLIC HEARINGS  
Pursuant to MGL ch  131, s 40 as amended and the Town of Lunenburg Wetlands Protection and 
Municipal Bylaws a public hearing on a Notice of Intent by Stephen Violette of Sunshine Nominee Trust 
for construction of a gravel driveway for accessing a future residential build site and agricultural land 
within the 100 and 50-foot buffer zones of a bordering vegetated wetland located at 157 Goodrich Street, 
(DEP # 208-1335, OpenGov .com Record ID # NOI -24-7). Greg Roy (Dillis & Roy),  representing the 
applicant, screen-shared the site plan and noted that this is a 3 acre parcel subdivided from 828 Lancaster 
Ave. The wetlands were delineated earlier in the summer and Mr. Violette would like to build a gravel 
driveway off Goodrich St. in order to access his agricultural fields. He has future plans to build a single 
family house , accessing the driveway, but is not doing that build now. The driveway will be entirely 
outside of the 30’ no-touch zone and no trees will be removed. Grading is associated with construction of 
the gravel driveway, with erosion control barrier between the wetlands and limit of work. They received a 
DEP file # 208-1335 with no comments. Wachusett Investment-new owners of 828 Lancaster Ave. - was 
issued a cease-and-desist from Conservation and the Building Commissioner. They have hired Dillis & 
Roy to file a NOI for that lot. Some fill from 828 Lancaster Ave. was moved to Stephen’s property  but 
none goes past where they are proposing to work or into the 30’ no -touch zone. Greg added that if the 
Commission approves this request, it would resolve the encroachment at 828 Lancaster Ave. Sophia asked 
if the driveway would be plowed in the winter and was concerned about use of de -icer. The 
Commissioners discussed run-off of gravel, grading and pitching of the driveway , and whether the 
property line could be moved . Greg responded that they can look into the possibility of moving the 
property line  and stated the area from the driveway to the wetlands is mostly flat .  Also they are not 
looking for any waivers. Sophia screen-shared and read a letter of concern from abutter Patricia Whitney 
(137 Goodrich St.), questioning the impact to her pond which is a Certified Wildlife Refuge. Mary noted 
===PAGE 2===
that they will need to continue the hearing since Ms. Whitney asked for an in -person meeting. Greg 
replied he would be willing to talk to her about the proposed work. Sophia noted that magnesium chloride 
is suggested as a low impact de -icer. Greg will look at the slope further in the property and Jenny’s 
suggestion about adding a lip to the edge to control run -off.  
Motion: Rich B., Second: Mike L. 
To continue the hearing on 157 Goodrich Street to 1/22/25.   
Roll Call V ote: Chris-aye, Jenny -aye, Rich-aye, Jeff -aye, Mike-aye and Mary -aye.  
 
CONTINUED PUBLIC HEARINGS  
Pursuant to MGL ch 131, s 40 as amended and the Town of Lunenburg Wetlands Protection and 
Municipal Bylaws a public hearing on a request to amend an Order of Resource Area Delineation by 
Christopher Anderson to remove a portion of the  Resource Area Delineation from the order due to 
maintenance per formed on a manmade detention basin located at 400 Leominster Shirley Road . 
(Continued from December 4th to January 8th.) Bill Hannigan (Hannigan Engineering) stated the Order 
of Resource Area Delineation was issued March 2023 and the appeal period has expired. He has emailed 
the DEP but has had no response. Mary suggested they reach out to DEP for guidance on the timeline. 
Bill requested a continuance to 1/22/25.  
Motion: Chris S., Second: Rich B.  
To continue the hearing on 400 Leominster Shirley Rd. to 1/22/25.  
Roll Call V ote: Chris-aye, Mike-aye, Jeff -aye, Rich -aye, Jenny -aye and Mary -aye. 
 
Pursuant to MGL ch 131, s 40 as amended and the Town of Lunenburg Wetlands Protection and 
Municipal Bylaws a public hearing on a Notice of Intent by D & A Lunenburg, LLC for construction of a 
stormwater management system within the 50- and 100-foot buffer zone, but outside of the 30-foot buffer 
zone of a Bordering Vegetative Wetland located at Electric Avenue and Pierce Avenue (Assessor’ s Map 
113, Parcel 18, Lot 2) (DEP # 208 -1332, OpenGov.com  Record ID # NOI -24-3). (Continued from 
December 4th to January 8th) Bill Hannigan (Hannigan Engineering) reported that  the final review was 
completed by the Peer Review but they had various comments coming from the Planner and other 
department heads. All these changes are outside of the jurisdiction of Conservation. Bill screen-shared the 
site plan and said they are asking for a waiver on the 40’ setback for 24 spaces. The Planner questioned if 
it was zoning issue and they will eliminate the 24 spaces, choosing to landscape the area instead. Bill is 
requesting the Commission approve the Order of Conditions based on the plans submitted which are not 
changing.  
Motion: Rich B., Second: Mike L.  
To approve the NOI, based on the plans submitted in September, as long as any changed plans are 
submitted to the office and there are no changes in the buffer zone.  
Roll Call V ote: Chris-aye, Mike-aye, Jenny-aye, Rich -aye, and Mary -aye.  Jeff abstained.  
 
Pursuant to MGL ch 131, s 40 as amended and the Town of Lunenburg Wetlands Protection and 
Municipal Bylaws a public hearing  on an Abbreviated Notice of Resource Area Delin eation by Kevin 
O’Brien at 390 Howard Street  (DEP # 208-1304). (Continued from December 4 th to January 8 th).  
No one present for this hearing.  
Motion: Mike L., Second: Chris S 
To continue the hearing on 390 Howard Street to 1/22/25.  
Roll Call V ote: Chris-aye, Mike-aye, Jeff -aye, Jenny-aye, Rich-aye, and Mary -aye. 
 
===PAGE 3===
Pursuant to MGL ch 131, s 40 as amended and the Town of Lunenburg Wetlands Protection and 
Municipal Bylaws a public hearing on a Notice of Intent by Nijal Hamberg for renovations to an existing 
single-family home, reconstruction of an existing deck, removal of four trees within the 50-100 foot buffer 
zone and res toration/replacement of an ex isting 50 sq.ft shed within the 50 -foot no -build zone of a 
bordering vegetated wetland located at 50 Pine Grove Road. (DEP # 208 -1336, OpenGov.com Record 
ID# NOI-24-6). (Continued from December 18th to January 8th) Greg Roy (Dillis & Roy) screen-shared 
the site plan with updates requested by the Commission as follows: The shed can be constructed outs ide 
of the 50’ no -structure zone, the 5 th tree to be removed was noted on the edge of the leach field, the 
staircase is noted outside of the 50’ zone, and the new deck will be composite with pressure treated 
framing. They found a poured concrete surface under the old deck, which will be removed and replaced 
by patio pavers  within the same footprint.  
Motion: Mike L., Second: Chris S 
To approve the NOI for 50 Pine Grove Road . 
Roll Call V ote: Rich-aye, Jenny-aye, Mike-aye, Chris-aye, and Mary -aye. Jeff abstained. 
 
Certificates of Compliance: 
---57 Round Road- Sophia reported there were 2 parts to this.  The 1st part (# 208-1322) was denied and 
they were told to come back with a new plan. The 2nd part (#208-1279) was for a simple wall at the edge 
of the lake and was completed per the plan. She advised to approve the C OC. 
Motion: Mike L., Second: Chris S. 
To approve the COC for 57 Round Road. 
Roll Call V ote: Rich-aye, Jenny -aye, Jeff-aye, Mike-aye, Chris -aye, and Mary -aye.  
---32 Pine Grove Road – Sophia recommended not approving since there were erosion issues fro m the 
100’ to 50’ and near the edge of the 30’  zone. She noted that they had cleared in the 100’ zone and it was 
trickling down. Sophia screen-shared a photo of erosion near the rock wall and the area seeded. Greg Roy 
(Dillis & Roy) reported that the erosion was outside of the 50’ zone  and work near the lake ha d been 
stabilized. They had hydroseeded but it did not take and nothing was disturbed within the 50’ zone.  
Motion: Rich B., Second: Jenny P.  
To deny the COC request for 32 Pine Grove Rd. and recommend the applicant come back when 
those areas are stabilized.  
Roll Call V ote: Chris-aye, Mike-aye, Jeff -aye, Jenny -aye, Rich -aye, and Mary -aye. 
---137 Flynn Road – Sophia advised approval, noting the plantings were installed very well with stakes.  
Motion: Rich B., Second: Jenny P. 
To approve the COC for 137 Flynn Road . 
Roll Call V ote: Chris-aye, Mike-aye, Jeff -aye, Rich-aye, Jenny-aye, and Mary -aye. 
 
VIOLATIONS & ENFORCEMENTS: 
---10 Mass. Ave.- Sophia reported that she sent a letter with a $300 fine to Smit Patel (owner) and the fine 
was paid. She screen -shared and read a letter from Mr. Patel requesting an extension to complete 
requirements and noted he was in Indi a for a critical family illness. He has engaged Dan Wells (LEC 
Environmental Consultants) to assist him with the site plan and NOI form. The Commission discussed the 
issue and agreed to issue a letter of requirements.  
Motion: Mike L., Second: Chris S.  
To issue a letter explaining the timeline we expect and requesting a response from his wetlands 
scientist on the schedule for addressing issues and when documentation will be submitted. 
Roll Call V ote: Rich-aye, Jenny -aye, Jeff -aye, Mike-aye, Chris -aye, and Mary -aye.  
---40 Fire Road 12 – Sophia and Mary met with Chris Reilly (Planner), who said he would talk to the 
Police Dept. and Scott Benjamin (abutter).  She has not had any updates but did a site visit earlier today  
===PAGE 4===
and noted the personal property is still on town property.  Mary and Jenny also visited the site on Saturday 
and spoke to both abutters. Mary noted the pavement had been removed but a small cord of wood, pallet 
and debris  were moved to within 5’ of the wetland . Scott Benjamin was very dismissive to them but 
Gordon Wu (other abutter) was very apologetic and gave them a history  of the issue. He stated that he 
paid to pull up the pavement, put down loam and seed it. Mary and Sophia met with Chris Reilly and  
discussed placing boulders as a permanent barrier. Chris will look into having the town place the boulders 
and will talk to Scott Benjamin. The Commission can approve the NOI now or wait until the boulders are 
in place and consider issuing a violation to Mr. Benjamin for moving his property to area immediately 
adjacent to wetlands. Sophia screen-shared a photo of the pile near the wetlands. Rich asked if the Select 
Board and Town Manager had been notified about the debris on town property and Mary replied no but 
will contact them. The Commission discussed whether to approve the NOI and decided to wait until the 
debris is cleaned up.    
---48 Meadow Lane – Sophia reported there was a carport shelter encroaching on 3 properties  and the 
shelter has been removed. However, she was informed of some dumping there which she will check into.  
---1168 Mass. Ave. Rear- Sophia sent a letter at the beginning of December to request they attend the 
meeting and also contacted Brian Marchetti in mid-December, who said he would talk to the owner. Brian 
responded that he talked to Jeff Powell, who asked for help with the process of resolving the permit 
issues. Brian will give an update at the 1/22 meeting. Sophia screen-shared a erial photos  of the site and 
Mary noted there was fill added that diverted the natural stream.  
 
NEW BUSINESS: 
Topics unknown 48 hours in advance of meeting  
---Anna Wilkins (North County Land Trust)- announced that the Land Trust, in conjunction with the 
Nashua River Watershed Assoc., are planning a Landowner Workshop in March to help them understand 
the land conservation options. The workshop will be geared to landowners in Lunenburg, Lancaster, 
Shirley and A yer. The program will go over Chapter 61 and other land management opportunities.  They 
are looking for a liaison in Lunenburg to help them connect to landowners and Mary suggested contacting 
the Open Space Committee. Anna noted that the state has a new Conservation Assistance Grant that gives 
money to towns to update their open space plans. Also, if possible, they are suggesting a donation to help 
co-sponsor the workshop.  
---Continuing Education for Commissioners – Sophia is looking to project how much money will be 
needed for classes in FY26.  Mary estimated needing 8 classes for new Commissioners and budgeting for 
2 classes each for other Commissioners, along with handbook access for all. She also suggested budgeting 
for flyers to be inserted into tax bills which Bob estimated at $1,200. Mary recommended putting a ¼ 
page ad in the Ledger explaining buffer zones along with a QR code to the website. The Commissioners 
discussed and agreed.  
---January Meeting Schedule- Next meeting is 1/22 and on 1/29 there will be a special meeting for the 
reports from the lake communities only.  
---Wildland Grants – Chris stated this is a global effort to protect 30% of the world’s land and water by 
2030 .  In this state, the Forest Reserve Grant Program is for land they want to acquire and the aim is to 
preserve areas of old growth , which have greater  bio-diversity and carbon storage. Wildlands have 
minimal activity with no development , motorized vehicles , logging  or agriculture . The grants come 
around August and would give 50% of the cost. Chris suggested it could be for preserving land near water 
storage such as Mulpus Brook. Lynne Man clarified that they do not want roads added but would allow 
hunting and passive recreation. They would need to get clarification on use of snowmobiles and Mary 
noted they would need to do public hearings. Currently, the Commission receives their monies from the 
Timber Rights  Fund.  
===PAGE 5===
---Farmland of Local Importance - Mary received an em ail from Al Avril from NRCS (Natural 
Resources Conservation Service).  The Federal Government  classifies 3 types of soil but there is a 4 th 
classification for local farmlands of importance. A federal grant sponsors a soil specialist, such as Al, to 
classify parcels as farmland of importance . The local Conservation Commission would sponsor that 
activity by endorsing his findings but there is no cost involved. This program may help some farmers to 
qualify for abatements. Mary reached out to Open Space, Agriculture Commission and the Select Board. 
---Conservation Budget Report  – will be posted in the meeting folder after the 15 th. 
---Paint Stewardship- Chris explained that this is a program for people to drop off paint. Mary believed 
paint could be recycled at the Devens program and Chris will check on that option.  
OLD BUSINESS: 
Update on Title Searches – no update  
April Earth Day Events – Mary is working on this and has reached out to Parks Commission and the 
Pollinator Habitat.  Mike will connect with the Pollinator Habitat also.  
 
Update if any on encroachment issues: Mary noted the Commission met in Executive Session with the 
Select Board on 1/7/25 concerning these issues.  
Open Space and Land Use:  
---Chapter 61 OpenGov Workflow – no update  
---Grants Update (LCC sponsored, award year 2023)- Mary reported the contract engineering firm has 
been working on the plans to raise NOIs on behalf of the Commission as the result of the parking areas. 
The Commission will review the plans before the y execute on those approvals.  
---Grants Update (LSR sponsored, award year 2022) – Mary submitted all the close out documentation 
on behalf of the Snow Riders in December. There were 251 hours of volunteer time and $5,000 equivalent 
usage of power e quipment. This equates to approximately  $16,000 back to the community.  
---Conservation Land Improvement- Mary reported there is ongoing work that Bob is coordinating  at 
the Laine property to improve the bridges so they will not float away in the spring and adding handrails.  
 
Hollis Road Residence : 
---Update on Maintenance Work- Mike reported the bags of invasive  plants were picked up by the 
DPW and taken care of. Also Cam’s Heating will be doing the annual service on 1/27. He announced that 
this is the 8th anniversary of the Pollinator Habitat and their newsletter noted thanks to the Commission.  
Committee Reports – Any Update 
---Open Space – Mary noted no update as they are meeting tonight.  
---Stormwater – Jenny reported no update  as they did not meet in Decembe r. 
 
Public Comment: Mary suggested the Commission think about a new representative to Open Space 
Committee. She also was contacted by a few people interested in filling the Commissioner vacancy  and 
she will contact the Select Board.  
 
Motion: Mike L., Second: Jenny P. 
To adjourn at 9:43pm.    
Roll Call V ote: Rich-aye, Jenny -aye, Jeff -aye, Chris-aye, Mike-aye, and Mary -aye.  
 
Respectfully submitted,  
 
Jane Ranley (Minutes Clerk)

Avenue, (OpenGov.com Record ID# NOI-24-10) (DEP # 208-1339).

RDA-24-8).

CONSERVATION COMMISSION EXECUTIVE SESSION 
Tuesday, February 25, 2025 6:00 PM  
AGENDA  
Remote via Zoom  
In accordance with the requirements of the Open Meeting Law, please be advised that this meeting is 
being recorded.  
The following information is also provided for members of the public that would like to participate in the 
meeting remotely: To Participate Remotely: From a computer, please use the link below to join the public 
meeting webinar: https://zoom.us/j/9091740347 or from a telephone, dial the toll free number (888) 475-
4499 and enter Webinar ID: 909-174- 0347 # If you have not registered with Zoom you will be asked for 
your name and an e-mail address. Note: No participant ID is needed, just press # when asked. Telephone 
users may dial *9 to request to speak after joining the meeting. Computer and app users may use the 
“raise hand” feature to request to speak. The agenda lists all the topics which may be discussed at the 
meeting and are those reasonably anticipated by the Chair. Votes may be taken as a result of these 
discussions. Not all items listed may in fact be discussed and other items not listed may also be brought 
up for discussion to the extent permitted by Open Meeting Law.  
6:00 PM CALL TO ORDER AND PLEDGE OF ALLEGIANCE  
I. JOINT EXECUTIVE SESSION Motion to Enter into Joint Executive Session with the Conservation 
Commission and not to re-enter into Open session pursuant to MGL c. 30 §21 (a)(3) to discuss strategy 
with respect to litigation if an open meeting may have detrimental effect on the litigating position of the 
Town, and the Chair of the Board so declares, namely Town of Lunenburg v. Latour, Worcester Superior 
Court Docket No. 2385CV01189  
II. ADJOURN 
 
Meeting schedule: Next meeting is March 5th, 2025, at 7:00 PM by Zoom platform

All hearings are pursuant to MGL Ch 131, s 40 as amended and the Town of Lunenburg Wetlands Protection and Municipal Bylaws: 
a. 199 Mass Ave – NOI-25-20 
A public hearing on a wetland and wetland buffer zone restoration project due to historic disturbance activities at the site. An open 
wetlands violation was issued on April 1, 2025 by the Lunenburg Conservation Commission. The 0-30'; 30'-50', and 50'-100' buffer zones 
will be restored using wetland seed mixes and a line of boulders and new maple trees spaced 50' on center along the limit of the 0-30' 'No 
Touch Zone'. 
b. 13 Birch Island Way- RDA-25-17
 
A public hearing on the construction of conventional wood with vinyl siding and asphalt roofing 3 season porch off the main entrance of 
the existing home.  The addition will be attached to the center of the home facing the lake with portions of the new footprint replacing the 
existing deck.  The room will be supported with a Helical Pier style foundation to avoid excavation or concrete work and provide minimal 
ground disturbance.  The addition will not extend towards the lake any further than the existing deck.

Road crossing over Mulpus Brook in order to mitigate further deterioration, prevent potential flooding caused by the existing undersized 
culvert, and meet the Massachusetts River and Stream Crossing Standards to the maximum extent practicable. The proposed culvert

TOWN OF LUNENBURG 
Lunenburg Conservation Commission

MINUTES 
Wednesday, 7 January 2026 – 7:00 PM | Zoom (Remote) 
1. CALL TO ORDER 
Roll Call: T. Doucette, A. Burnap, M. Wilson, M. Larouche, J. Pewtherer. 
Absent: C. St. Germain, R, Bursch 
Call to Order at 7pm by J. Pewtherer 
2. APPOINTMENTS 
a. 7:10 PM 49 Cliffview T errace CAD-25-25  <Video link> 
A Public Hearing on a Conservation Administrator’s Determination regarding removal of 11 dead hemlock trees. They were 
probably killed by HWA over the last decade. These trees are a safety hazard to persons and property. The trees will be 
selectively remov ed without disturbing the surrounding trees and vegetation.  
 
Dick Fisher presented th e project.  Herlin's comments were read into the record  as follows: 
(11) hemlock trees were observed to be dead, exhibiting  extensive branch dieback, lack of live foliage, and overall 
structural decline. The condition of these trees is consistent with long -term impacts from Hemlock Woolly Adelgid 
(HW Eleven A) infestation, which has affected hemlock populations throughout the region over the past decade. The 
dead hemlocks were identified throughout the wooded slope and along the shoreline edge. Due to their height, 
location, and deteriorated condition, these trees pose a safety hazard to persons and property, particularly durin g 
wind, snow, or ice events.  
Motion: M. Wilson, to approve as presented with the following stipulations:  
1. Tree removal shall be limited to the eleven (11) dead hemlock trees identified during the site visit.  
2. Trees shall be selectively removed to minimize disturbance to surrounding trees, understory 
vegetation, and soils.   
3. Stumps and root systems shall remain in place unless otherwise authorized.  
4. No equipment staging, stockpiling, or debris disposal shall occur within resource areas.  
5. Any additional tree removal or disturbance beyond what is authorized will require further review by 
the Conservation Agent or Conservation Commission  
Second  T. Doucette 
Vote: A. Burnap – AYE M. Wilson – AYE M. Larouche - AYE T. Doucette – AYE 
 J. Pewtherer – AYE     
 
b. 7:15 PM T om Anelons to discuss trail grooming on conservation area trails   <Video link>

c. 41 Hilltop Ln: CAD-25-23  
Applicant not presen t.  Herlin’s note read:  
Four trees were observed to be dead, exhibiting extensive branch dieback and lack of live foliage. The trees appear 
structurally compromised and no longer viable. Due to their location and condition, these trees pose a potential risk of 
failure, with the likelihood of falling toward nearby st ructures and causing property damage.  
Tree removal shall be limited to the four dead trees identified  during the site visit. Stumps shall remain in place and shall not 
be ground or excavated. All work shall be conducted using best management practices to minimize disturbance to 
surrounding soils and vegetation. No equipment staging, stockpiling, or debris  disposal shall occur within resource areas. 
Any additional tree removal beyond what is authorized will require further review by the Conservation Agent or Conservation 
Commission. 
 
M. Wilson noted the provided pictures  and the narrative do not fully synch.  A detailed diagram and listing of damaged  trees 
would be helpful.

i. 98 Horizon Island Rd: CAD-25-26 
Herlin’s note s:   
Tree with the split trunk is deemed  a hazard tree and presents an imminent risk to public safety and property. Removal of 
this tree is warranted to prevent potential injury or damage.  
In addition to the primary hazard tree, several nearby mature trees were observed to contain dead, rotted, and/or 
compromised branches. These branches appear structurally unsound and pose an ongoing safety concern, particularly 
during wind or snow events.  
Additionally, while the tree crew is mobilized on site, the removal of dead, rotted, or hazardous branches from adjacent tree s 
is considered reasonable and appropriate as a preventative safety measure, provided that such work is limited to pruning and 
does not result in additional ground disturbance or expanded tree removal beyond what is necessary to address safety 
concerns.  
Motion: M. Wilson, to approve removal of tree removal and damaged branches as proposed 
with the following stipulations:  
1. Tree removal and pruning shall be conducted using best management practices 
to minimize disturbance to surrounding soils and resource areas.  
2. No staging, equipment storage, or debris stockpiling shall occur within resource 
areas. 
3. All work shall be limited to the removal of the identified hazard tree and 
selective pruning of visibly dead or rotted branches on nearby trees.  
4. No additional tree removal is authorized without further review by the 
Conservation Agent or Conservation Commission  
Second  M. Larouche 
Vote: A. Burnap – AYE M. Wilson – AYE M. Larouche - AYE T. Doucette – AYE

vi. 715 Reservoir Rd  
8. NEW BUSINESS  
a. Topics Unknown – 48 Hrs Advance of Meeting: None 
b. By-Law Discussion:  
i. The cost structure for consulting should be reviewed. M. Wilson feels this should be in regulation and not in the by-laws. 
c. MACC Training Takeaways: None  
d. Lunenburg April Earth Days  
i. General discussion.  T. Doucette indicated he would be willing to take the lead. 
e. Grant updates  
i. M. Wilson reviewed the status of the next phase of grants. She and J. Pewtherer have taken on a more proactive role as Bob 
focuses on his health. 
ii. The conservation budget would have to cover 20% of overall construction costs at Table Rock, which would be a not-to-
exceed amount of $10,287. The Timber Rights fund currently has over $41,000. 
iii. The conservation budget would have to cover 20% of overall construction costs for Raino, which would be a not-to-exceed 
amount of $9,462. 
Motion: M. Larouche, to approve submission of grant proposals as presented with the caveat:  
• Funding from Timber Rights and not exceeding $19,749.  
Second  R, Bursch 
Vote: T. Doucette – AYE R. Bursch – AYE M. Wilson - AYE A. Burnap – AYE 
 M. Larouche – AYE J. Pewtherer - AYE    
 
 
9. OLD BUSINESS  
a. Conservation budget:  
i. Monthly oil bill pre-approval  
Motion: M. Larouche, to approve a purchase order for 123 Hollis monthly oil service not to 
exceed $1,500.  
Second  M. Wilson 
Vote: T. Doucette – AYE R. Bursch – AYE M. Wilson - AYE A. Burnap – AYE 
 M. Larouche – AYE J. Pewtherer - AYE    
 
b. Encroachment Issues: None 
c. Hollis Rd Residence: None 
d. Committee Reports – Any Update:  
i. Master Plan Steering Committee: Final meeting is on February 25.   
ii. Open Space: No update 
iii. Storm Water: Going to Finance and Select Board.  
10. COMMUNICATIONS / PUBLIC COMMENT: Discussed how the Town gets notified when we pass ethics course. 
11. UPCOMING MEETING SCHEDULE: 
a. Wednesday 2/4 at 7:00PM by Zoom 
12. ADJOURN 
Motion: R. Bursch, to adjourn at  
Second  M. Wilson 
Vote: T. Doucette – AYE R. Bursch – AYE M. Wilson - AYE A. Burnap – AYE 
 M. Larouche – AYE J. Pewtherer - AYE    
 
===PAGE 4===
   
 
Respectfully submitted, 
Michael Gale  - Admin Asst.

MINUTES 
Wednesday, 3 June 2026 – 7:00 PM | Zoom (Remote) 
1. CALL TO ORDER 
Roll Call: J. Pewtherer, R. Bursch, M. Larouche, T. Doucette 
Absent: M. Wilson, A. Burnap (joined at 29:17 minutes into the meeting) 
Call to Order at 7pm by J. Pewtherer 
2. APPOINTMENTS: None 
3. ANNOUNCEMENTS 
a. Public Comment. (No comments from members of the public.) 
i. Pollinator Habitat Update 
M. Larouche reviewed the May 17 event, sharing photos of plants and butterflies. It was basically an 
opportunity to organize for the upcoming season. J. Pewtherer noted the Habitat is located at 123 Hollis Road, 
is open to the public during daylight hours, and provides free parking 
ii. Drought Declaration: DEP May 22 
DEP raised the drought level to level 2 (significant drought) for the Connecticut River Valley, Central and 
Nantucket Counties. We are part of Central County. They want all regions within that scope to minimize overall 
use of water, follow local water restrictions, fix indoor leaks such as toilets, faucets, showers, and that larger 
buildings and businesses should conduct audits to identify any areas of leakage. DEP wants people to limit 
washing hard surfaces like sidewalks, patios, driveways, and sidings. 
b. Minutes of Previous Meetings:  
05/20/2026 
Motion: T. Doucette, to approve the minutes of the 5/20 meeting. 
Second M. Larouche 
Vote: M. Larouche – AYE R. Bursch – AYE T. Doucette - AYE J. Pewtherer – AYE 
 
4. NEW PUBLIC HEARINGS 
All hearings are pursuant to MGL Ch 131, s 40 as amended and the Town of Lunenburg Wetlands Protection and 
Municipal Bylaws: 
a. 146 Youngs Road: NOI-26-18 
Notice of Intent by Nichols Excavating, LLC for the replacement of a failed septic system 
Ryan Proctor (Dillis & Roy) represented the applicant. The existing sewer line comes out of the existing house. 
The new system will go in the same general location. The driveway will be relocated outside the 50-foot zone. 
Due to grading, the new septic will need a pump chamber. Breakout grading will require a retaining wall. Erosion 
controls will be used. Board of Health approval is still needed as they are within 100 feet of a well. Still waiting 
for the DEP number. 
Motion: R. Bursch, to continue to the June 17 meeting. 

===PAGE 2===
   
 
Second T. Doucette 
Vote: M. Larouche – AYE R. Bursch – AYE T. Doucette - AYE A. Burnap – AYE 
 J. Pewtherer – AYE      
 
b. 507 West Townsend Road: NOI-26-19 
Notice of Intent by Jahnke Construction LLC for the construction of a single-family home with a wetland 
crossing   
Zach Mann (New England Environmental Design) represented the applicant. New construction on a large 
property. The area was previously disturbed about 8-10 years ago. A long driveway is needed but will have to 
enter over an existing stream. The DEP number has not been received. Minor grading in the 50-foot buffer, and 
the house is in the 100-foot zone. T. Doucette wanted the reason a common driveway was not possible.  Z. Mann 
said the neighbor did not want a common driveway. A parallel driveway was not considered due to the existing 
pipe. R. Bursch wanted a planting schematic, construction detail for the temporary disturbance, and a planting 
demarcation to protect the 30-foot zone. He also wondered why the house cannot be outside a buffer zone when 
there is over 28 acres. Z. Mann said it was simply a client preference. H. Jaime suggested this could also improve 
the large driveway requirement.  
Motion: M. Larouche, continue to the June 17 meeting, addressing the following: 
1. Replication detail on the disturbed area 
2. 30-foot buffer planting demarcation native plants 
3. Move the structure outside the buffer zone 
4. Locate the staging area on the map 
Second T. Doucette 
Vote: M. Larouche – AYE R. Bursch – AYE T. Doucette - AYE A. Burnap – AYE 
 J. Pewtherer – AYE      
 
5. CONTINUED PUBLIC HEARINGS 
a. 1625 Massachusetts Avenue: NOI-26-10 (Continued from 05/06/26) 
Notice of Intent by the Town of Lunenburg Conservation Commission for the construction of an ADA parking and 
re-construction of an existing hiking trail at Cowdery Nature Center. Continued to 06/17 
b. 860 New West Townsend Road: NOI-26-1 (Continued from 05/20/26) 
Notice of Intent by Daniel & Rachel Brackett for a common driveway to provide access to four residential lots 
Ryan Proctor (Dillis & Roy) represented the applicants, who were also present. Covers 3 properties. Will involve 
4 townhomes each with 5 units using a common driveway. Applications are still before the Planning Board. 
Comments from the peer review and DEP have been submitted. Many peer review questions were stormwater 
related. Property had a forest cutting plan 10 years ago, and the owners now want to develop the property. No 
more cutting will take place. H. Jaime noted that we may want to wait until DEP has received the responses. R. 
Bursch suggested waiting until all Planning Board approvals are received.  
Motion: R. Bursch, to continue to the June 17 meeting, addressing the following: 
1. Wait for DEP to review responses 
2. Wait for all Planning Board approvals to be complete 
Second M. Larouche 
Vote: M. Larouche – AYE R. Bursch – AYE A. Burnap - AYE J. Pewtherer – AYE 
 T. Doucette - Abstain      
 
 
c. 56 Ruth Street: NOI-26-16 (Continued from 05/20/26) 
===PAGE 3===
   
 
Notice of Intent by Benjamin Builders for the construction of common driveway entrance with related drainage 
basin and site grading 
The applicant requested a continuance. 
Motion: M. Larouche, continue to the June 17 meeting at applicant’s request. 
Second R. Bursch 
Vote: M. Larouche – AYE R. Bursch – AYE T. Doucette - AYE A. Burnap – AYE 
 J. Pewtherer - AYE

working with USDA regarding the farm plan, waiting for the USDA response. DEP conducted a site walk but 
has not sent the report.

H. Jaime is in touch with the owner. 
b. Emergency Certifications: 
8. NEW BUSINESS 
a. Updates: 
i. Hickory Hills Lake: 2026 Treatment Plan 
Dominic Meringolo (SOLitude) presented the treatment plan. 5 areas of the lake (25 acres) identified for

Motion: T. Doucette, to approve the treatment plan as presented. 
Second M. Larouche

 J. Pewtherer - AYE      
ii. CMMCP- 1147 Mass Ave 
The Central Mass Mosquito Control Plan will be digging a ditch as maintenance and provided this notice as a 
courtesy. This is free to the property owner. 
b. Topics Unknown – 48 Hrs. Advance of Meeting 
H, Jaime received notice that several large trees were planted at 715 Reservoir Rd inside the 30-foot buffer. She 
conducted a site visit. A six-month inspection would be in December, so she suggested a spring checkup. The 
Commission agreed.  
c. By-Law Discussion: None 
9. OLD BUSINESS 
a. Conservation budget:  
===PAGE 4===
   
 
Final and proposed budgets will be ready for the June 17 meeting. She reviewed the work required to get to this 
state.  
b. Encroachment Issues: None, but Bob Pease has an old list that he will review with H. Jaime. 
c. Hollis Rd Residence: 
Both cars are now gone. The trampoline has been dismantled and placed on the side of the road. Sumac and 
bittersweet plants have been removed. M. Larouche had circulated a proposal to everyone. A cleaning company 
was identified and researched. Power and water will need to be restored. There is still a lot of junk to be cleaned 
up. Bill Bernard from DPW will drop off a dumpster. 
Cleaning quote includes carpet cleaning ($216), touchable services ($1,872), vapor order counteractant ($122), 
supplies ($100), heavy pet spot removal ($95) for a subtotal of $2,405. This would come from the “Haws” fund. 
Motion: M. Larouche, to approve $2,411.25 from the “Haws” fund to hire a cleaning company 
for the 123 Hollis Road property. 
Second T. Doucette 
Vote: M. Larouche – AYE R. Bursch – Abstain T. Doucette - AYE A. Burnap – AYE 
 J. Pewtherer - AYE      
 
d. Committee Reports – Any Update 
i. Master Plan Steering Committee: No update. T. Doucette will reach out to Kim Duffy for status. 
ii. Open Space: Meeting next week 
iii. Storm Water: Figuring out how to approach commercial properties. 
10. COMMUNICATIONS / PUBLIC COMMENT 
1. J. Pewtherer noted that Brad Coons attended the entire meeting. He was impressed by how prepared the 
applicants were and the level of detail discussed. He is more interested in doing trail stewardship or an associate 
position (non-voting positions). 
2. T. Doucette attended a trail walk of Lane property regarding invasive management. Seed collection was 
discussed (an individual for personal business or open collection for anyone). The library has seed packets that 
can be picked up.

 J. Pewtherer - AYE

Michael Gale - Admin Asst.

b. Amended Order of Conditions: None 
c. Extension Request Permits: 
757 Chase Road 208-1217 CEXT-26-1   
J. Almonte of Land Design Collabrative representing Empire Management.  J. Almonte gave an overview of the 
proposed project and a request for an extension of the existing Order of Conditions.  The project was approved in 
2001, all permits (Planning, Zoning, etc. ) have all expired.  Nothing has changed and all permitting requirements 
have been resubmitted. 
H. Jamie stated that although her comments are not a part of this discussion, H. Jamie was at the site for a site 
inspection and to the right side of the current structure there is an additional building where she observed vehicles 
being washed on the property.  The wash water appeared to flow toward the adjacent wetland resource area.  
While H Jamie was there she met with chair M. Proctor from the Board of Health, who had presented on site with 
the same concern.  Staff indicated that this had been their practice for many years, even though there was a 
violation issued last year and a cease and desist was issued, which was completely disregarded.  There were 
soaps, detergents, grease, oils and other pollutants that have the potential to impact adjacent resource areas. Also I 
want to preface this when considering an extension of an existing Order of Condition, if the property owner 
cannot abide by current standards of the Wetlands Protection Act, it leads me to believe that they lose their right to 
receive their extension until they  remediated these ongoing issues.   I recommend that the Commission direct the 
property owner to immediately discontinue this activity.  If the practice continues after notification, I recommend 
that the Commission authorize the issuance of a formal Notice of Violation and pursue appropriate enforcement 
action.  J. Almonte assured the Commission that all car washing activities on the property have ceased.  A letter 
will be sent of a pre-warning of enforcement if activities continue. 
Brian Lafferty of Empire Management, owner of the property, was present.  The dealerships rent the property.  A 
statement that this will not happen again, there will be no more car washing at the facility.  H. Jamie stated that 
she was at the property and she witnessed the washing of cars.  Topography is such that the highest point washes 
to the lowest point.  Car washing was at the highest point washing into the lowest point, which is a wetland.  H. 
Jamie respectfully stated that she witnessed the car washing and spoke directly to the entity washing the cars.  
Violations are sent to the name and address listed in the public records of the Town of Lunenburg. 
Motion: M. Wilson, to approve the extension to Order of Conditions for 3 years  
Second M. Larouche 
Vote: M. Wilson – AYE M. Larouche – AYE A. Burnap - AYE J. Pewtherer – AYE 
 T. Doucette - AYE     
  
d. Certificates of Compliance: None 
e. Notice of Project Change:  None 
8. ENFORCEMENT 
a. Violations, Enforcement Actions, and Hearings: 
i. 1168 Mass Ave Rear 
H. Jaime notice from DEP.  They advised the Commission should move forward with an Enforcement Order to 
best address the situation.  They are requesting that the Enforcement Order require the following:  An 
evaluation of the property for all the wetland resource areas and reviews that quantify all the altered wetland 
resource areas, the evaluation to be conducted by a qualified wetland professional and the evaluation should 
===PAGE 5===
   
 
include subservice soil analysis of sufficient depth throughout the site, including areas along the stream and 
areas that confirm through the ORAD.  DEP recommends that this deliverable is due no later than 30 days of 
the issuance of the Enforcement Order.  They are also asking for the submission of a restoration plan, which 
includes surveyed topo plans and a description of the unpermitted work and the areas to be restored.  They want 
written confirmation of the intent to comply with the Order within 5 business days of the date the Enforcement 
Order is issued.  They recommend that the Commission review the bvw delineations associated with the ORAD 
so we can confirm the bank resource area associated with the intermittent streams and that it is still present on 
the property and should be included in the evaluation.  The 2 year time frame to inforce violations is nearing the 
end.  We have reached out to the property owner numerous times, it took a long time to get DEP out there and 
made numerous attempts in various ways to contact the property owner without a response.  They seem to 
wants to work on this but are not being pushed enough so that they are present something to resolve the issues.  
DEP wants an Enforcement Order issued and H. Jamie is in favor of doing same. 
 
Motion: M. Larouche, to Issue Enforcement Order with DEP Recommendations 
Second T. Doucette 
Vote: M. Wilson – Recuse M. Larouche – AYE A. Burnap - AYE T. Doucette – AYE 
 J. Pewtherer - AYE    
  
b. Emergency Certifications: None 
c.  
9. NEW BUSINESS 
a. Updates: None 
Commission Open Discussion 
M. Wilson – Mass Recreational Trails Grant Program  is a good source of funding for the Commission to do 
conservation land improvements. This year we applied for two separate grants.  One for the Table Rock parking 
area and the permitting work that had been done by Mass Recreational Trails and one for installation of the Rayno 
property parking area with engineering and permitting done by Recreational Trails.  Unfortunately we were not 
awarded grant money.  It appears grants funding is down this year.  They had 121 applications and funded 50.  A 
lot of the ones funded were funding for rail trails and bigger ticket projects.  Federal Funding has gone away.  We 
can apply again next year.  Rayno property might be a property for state funding grants.  T. Doucette stated he 
appreciates the work done although grant funding was not approved.  Mention was made that M. Wilson did most 
of the work on grants. 
b. By-Law Discussion 
H. Jaime continued to a later meeting. 
10. OLD BUSINESS

i. Massey Tract

helping the property owner.  The Director of North County Land Trust and Town Counsel talked regarding what 
is required by the Town.  Town Counsel advised that they had a conversation with and our Town Manager J. 
Warren who is differing to the Commission with regard to a question regarding the deed.  North County Land 
trust is requesting a commitment of 25% of the cost of a Title Search and Title Insurance, of which 75% will be 
reimbursed by the Federal Government.  According to Town Counsel, the total cost of Title Insurance and Title 
Search is below $5,000 of which the Commission would be responsible for 25%. M. Wilson suggested that 
these funds come from the Timber Rights Account.   
 
Motion: M. Wilson, to approve 25% of the funds necessary for title search and title insurance for 
Massey property as a part of Forest Legacy Program (through North County Land Trust)

Municipal Bylaws: 
  
a. 15 White Street: NOI-26-23  
Notice of Intent by Carlos Silvera for the repair / upgrade of failed septic system 
Luke Rotti was present, as the soil evaluator and septic installer.  The engineer was not available.  Mr. Rotti was 
present for the PERC test. Mr. Rotti is familiar with the plan, and a more detailed plan was uploaded recently.  
When members of the commission did the site walk, it was difficult to make where on the property the septic was 
going, so the uploaded plan is much better. 
 
Mr. Rotti stated that the existing septic system is in the rear of the house; the proposed system will be on the side 
of the house at a higher elevation and as far away from the wetlands as possible, which are at the rear of the 

===PAGE 2===
   
 
house.  H. Jamie stated that she met the engineer at the site in late fall.  They worked together to find the best and 
most appropriate side for the new system.  There is no DEP number as of yet on this project

H. Jamie is asking for a continuance on behalf of the applicant to continue to resolve issues. 
Motion: T. Doucette, continue to August 19th. 
Second M. Laroche 
Vote: T. Doucette - AYE M. Larouche – AYE A. Burnap - AYE J. Pewtherer – AYE 
     
 
b. 860 New West Townsend Road NOI-26-1 Continued from 7/1/26 
Notice of Intend by Daniel & Rachel Brackett for a common driveway to provide access to four residential lots 
Ryan Proctor present for the owner who stated there have been no outstanding questions or comments from the 
commission at this point.  R. Proctor stated that with regard to the Planning Board, they have met the requirement 
of review engineers, Graves Engineering.  Currently, the only outstanding issue as submitting a draft HOA 
document to be reviewed by town counsel.  R. Proctor stated that the letter from Graves was uploaded to the 
portal as well as the most recent plan, which has no changes.  If there are no further questions from this 
commission, a request was made to close this hearing. 
T. Doucette asked what the HOA is in regard to. R. Proctor stated that the HOA covers basically everything and 
has been submitted to town counsel.  These are townhouses.  The Planning Board is primarily concerned with the 
common driveway themselves including snow plowing and maintenance.  T. Doucette asked if the draft HOA was 
uploaded.  R. Proctor was unsure of it was uploaded to the portal as it is a draft.  If it has not been, he will upload 
the HOA draft document.   M. Larouche stated that R. Proctor has not gotten a response from the Planning Board 
on the latest submission but he could with regard to the both the site plan and HOA.  R Proctor stated that the 
presented the revised plans and there were no questions.  He addressed comments from the fire department.  
Outstanding question related to the HOA and thickness of the pavement.  There are no other outstanding design 
question of any nature.  H. Jamie stated that she had concerns about a proposed HOA.  If the Selectboard denies 
it, it serves no purpose.  R. Proctor stated that the operation agreements will point toward the operation, the HOA 
agreement will point toward the operation and maintenance manual, which is included in the stormwater report, 
which you did receive a copy of.  All are those are outlined and I will make sure you have all the latest drainage 
reports.  H. Jamie stated that if all of that is in place, it is in everyone’s interest to move forward. 
Motion: T. Doucette, to Approve Notice of Intent for Common Driveway

e. 701 Reservoir Road NOI-26-21 (Continued from 7/1/26) 
Notice of Intent by the Reservoir Recreational Trust for the construction of a new concrete boat ramp on Lake 
Shirley at Shady Point 
R. Proctor (Dillis & Roy) was present for the applicant who stated that a DEP number was received with 
comments relating to quantifying impacts.  R. Proctor stated that in addition to the boat ramp, they were also 
proposing some shoreline stabilization improvements by way of a wall.  They are soliciting quotes for a wildlife 
habitat evaluation.  This is required when you alter greater than 50 linear feet of a shoreline.  Also, there was a 
question as to the impervious area for the project as it related to stormwater.  R. Proctor stated that the comments 
were just received and he will be working through them and submit revised materials.  R. Proctor stated that he is 
asking for a continuance.  Rich Pearson, 75 Ruth Street was present through zoom who stated that he is totally in 
favor of this project.  The owner is generous to the lake community in many ways and to the larger Lunenburg 
community as well.  H. James stated that she has received two letter of support.  One from Andrew Storm and one 
from Joanna Bilotta, who is the president of the Lake Shirley Improvement Corporation.  Also a letter from Carl 
Luck with concerns.  Andrew Storm, 715 Flat Hill Road, was present and stated that he supports the project as 
stated in his letter (will be upload to the portal).  The boat ramp is an invaluable resource to the promote access to 
the lake.  Carl Luck was present at the meeting.  C. Luck stated that he submitted his concerns by letter.  They do 
have a commonality with DEP concerns but I do support his project.  The ramp does need to be improved and it is 
his hope that it is done in the best way possible.  Mr. Luck would like to see pervious pavers used for the ramp.  
At the last meeting it appears to have a significant increase in the impervious area within 50 feet of the lake.  R. 
Proctor stated that they will be addressing the impervious question and will be coming back with an answer to that 
and will be formalizing same in a letter.  D. DeFazion of 31 Fire Road was present at the meeting and stated that 
she has seen several commercial or State boat ramps and they are all concrete and has concerns about pavers 
being able to handle the traffic.  This is a revamp of the existing ramp and due to wear and tear needs upgrading.  
D. Defazon is also in favor of this project. Chair Pewtherer stated that she wanted to echo the sentiment that she is 
hearing that the applicant has worked really hard to protect the trees and shoreline in the past and is continuing to 
do so now.  Carl Luck stated that in his letter he has concerns about power loading boats, which rips up the bottom 
of the lake and would like to see some restriction on power loading boats.  Mr. Luck is also concerned about the 
amount of solid wall around the lake.  Most of the walls have been stone walls so that the habitat is not disturbed.  
C. Luck stated that the in the past the Commission has pushed people toward stone walls.  J. Pewtherer stated for 
clarification that power loading is using the boat’s motor to get the boat on the trailer rather than using a winch.  
Chris Coffin, owner, stated that they do not allow power loading and we do have signage that we don’t allow it 
but we can’t catch everyone.  With regard to walls, there are many walls being put in with blocks, two recent ones 
in the channel.  C. Luck asked for information on those walls.  H. Jamie stated that that is not relevant to this 
hearing and C. Luck can request information outside this hearing.  R. Proctor is asking to continue the hearing 
until August 5th. 
Motion: T. Doucette, to continue to 8/5 
Second M. Larouche 
Vote: J. Pewtherer - AYE M. Larouche – AYE A. Burnap - AYE T. Doucette – AYE 
===PAGE 4===

g. 100 Laurel Lane:  RDA-26-7 (Continued from 7/1/26) 
Request for Determination of Applicability by Brian Pollard for the decommissioning of a septic system and 
installation of grinder pump 
Brian Pollard was present at the meeting who stated that they are going to decommission the existing septic 
system and will be installing a grinder pump in place of the septic tank and pump to connect to the town sewer 
system.  An ADU will also be tied in.  The existing septic system with be abandoned with the tank being pumped 
and filled.  There will be erosion control.  H. Jamie stated that she has worked with the applicant on this project 
and believes this is the best solution as they are tying into town sewage.  Applicant needs to get conservation 
approval so they can move forward with the DPW portion of this project.  H. Jamie would recommend a negative 
determination on this application.

Commission Open Discussion 
b. By-Law Discussion 
H. Jamie is proposing amending the Wetlands Protection Bylaw (Article XXI) – specifically the section governing 
filing fees to more clearly state: 
- The Conservation Commission shall establish and amend its fee schedule by vote of the Commission; and 
- The adopted fee schedule shall be maintained by the Commission and made available to the public.   
Additionally, to remove actual dollar amounts included in the bylaw and instead reference the fee schedule 
adopted by the Commission.  J. Pewtherer stated that now it is very hard for the Commission to change fees 
because it requires a Town Meeting vote.  This would enable the Commission to change fees with a vote of the 
Commission.  M. Larouche stated his support for the change in bylaw and fees schedule.  M. Pewtherer stated she 
would like to continue this discussion until there is a full board. 
 
Kim Duffy of 1479 Mass Avenue was present at the meeting.  She shared that she was recently at the Cowdry and 
the red trail along the river is completely overgrown.  She is suggesting that if a group is looking for a project, the 
area needs to be cleared out.  There is also a need for a foot bridge to cross the water.  J. Pewtherer asked when 
she was there and K. Duffy answered July 4th.  J. Pewtherer asked that K. Duffy send an email to H Jamie and 
copy the board.  There are other groups willing to do projects.   
9. OLD BUSINESS 
a. Conservation budget:  
Eagle Scout Project 
J. Pewtherer stated that although she would prefer not to make the Eagle Scouts wait, she feels it is important to 
have a full board to vote on the project.  T. Doucette stated that he is in favor of this project and providing funds 
to support it.  J. Pewtherer stated that she does have some reservations with this project.  In the past, Eagle 
Scouts came with a fully complete project where their funding sources are coming from, what materials they 
are using.  Discussion continued for a full board  
b. Encroachment Issues: None to discuss.

In accordance with the requirements of the Open Meeting Law, please be advised that this meeting is being audio / video recorded. The agenda lists 
all the topics which may be discussed at the meeting, and those reasonably anticipated by the Chair. Votes may be taken from these discussions. Not 
all items may be discussed, and other items may be raised to the extent permitted by Open Meeting Law. Please be advised that the Town of 
Lunenburg utilizes Zoom platform as host for its hybrid and remote meetings; that the Zoom platform utilizes AI technology, known as “AI 
Companion,” to create audio transcriptions of meetings to aid in the subsequent preparation of meeting minutes; and that AI Companion is activated 
immediately upon launching the Zoom application. You are hereby notified that this AI technology may capture audio prior to the start of the 
meeting and/or following the conclusion of the meeting. Join Zoom Meeting: https://us02web.zoom.us/J/87526214314. Meeting ID: 875 2621
